#2950. Down 60% in 2026, Is SoundHound Stock a Buy With Sustained Profitability Around the Corner?

SoundHound stock has been crushed this year, but better days could be ahead.

SoundHound AI (SOUN -3.06%) shares have been decimated this year after the company decided to acquire troubled LivePerson, with the deal being completed in September. While LivePerson's market cap was small, the company carried significant debt, which SoundHound AI quickly retired through an equity raise. With that now behind the company and management targeting sustained profitability in the second half of next year, now could be the time to buy this beaten-down AI stock.

Following the completion of its acquisition of LivePerson, SoundHound AI announced that it is transitioning its revenue model. It will reduce its reliance on licensing deals and shift its focus to a recurring-revenue, hosted-solution model. This will be led by its new voice-native agentic AI orchestration platform, OASYS.

The company said its OASYS platform is the merging of technology from six pioneering companies that create a leading voice, language intelligence, and agentic AI orchestration solution. The platform is AI model-agnostic and can layer over humans to handle as much work through AI as possible. Meanwhile, it's partnered with outsourcers, resellers, and cloud marketplaces with established relationships, enabling it to reach thousands of new enterprise customers.

SoundHound AI, meanwhile, is already working to upsell the large customer base it acquired with LivePerson. It has reached out to LivePerson's 90 largest enterprise customers, which account for over $100 million in annual recurring revenue (ARR), and says the response has been "overwhelmingly positive." It said its acquisition has removed LivePerson customers' main sticking points, which were questions about the company's financial health and the lack of an enterprise-grade voice and agent AI solution.

Overall, for Q3, SoundHound AI's revenue jumped 48.5% to $62.4 million, fueled by strong product demand and $14 million in revenue from LivePerson. That was below the $65.8 million analyst consensus, as revenue was affected by the transition in the revenue model.

The company is still not yet profitable. Its adjusted EPS came in at a $0.05 loss compared to $0.03 loss in the year-ago period. Adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA), meanwhile, was a loss of $20.7 million versus a loss of $14.5 million a year ago.

Its gross margin fell 850 basis points year over year to 34.1%, while its adjusted gross margin sank 900 basis points to 50.3%. However, SoundHound AI is optimistic about future gross margin expansion, as it deliberately chooses not to renew acquired negative-gross-margin contracts worth around $15 million and de-emphasizes low-growth lines of business. Meanwhile, the company has identified $120 million in annual cost savings following the LivePerson deal, which led it to forecast sustained adjusted EBITDA profitability starting in the second half of 2027.

With the LivePerson deal closed, the company now expects to hit full-year 2026 revenue of between $250 million and $260 million. For 2027, it projected revenue of between $350 million and $400 million, which compared to a prior forecast for revenue of at least $350 million.

SoundHound AI's stock has struggled mightily over the past couple of years, and the LivePerson acquisition, the roll-off of acquired negative-gross-margin contracts, and the shift to a recurring revenue business model make things a bit messy going forward. In that mess, though, it looks like there is a good opportunity for SoundHound AI to use LivePerson's customer base to become a premier player in the customer service and call center space with its OASYS platform.

SOUN PS Ratio (Forward 1y) data by YCharts

The stock's valuation has come down significantly, and it now trades at a forward price-to-sales (P/S) ratio of 5 times 2027 analyst estimates. Now, the company will have to execute and improve its gross margin and profitability to justify that multiple, but the stock is starting to get interesting as a rebound candidate for next year. As such, I'd be willing to take a flier on this speculative growth stock down at these levels.

#2944. Better Healthcare ETF: Invesco's PJP vs. Simplify's PINK

Concentrated pharma exposure delivered 31% returns over one year, while active healthcare management achieved 18%.

Comparing the Simplify Health Care ETF (PINK +0.13%) and Invesco Pharmaceuticals ETF (PJP +0.41%) reveals distinct approaches to healthcare, with PINK focusing on broad innovation through active management and PJP offering concentrated exposure to the U.S. pharmaceutical industry.

Investors seeking healthcare exposure must often choose between the stability of established pharmaceutical giants and the growth potential of medical technology. The Simplify Health Care ETF uses an active strategy to find innovators, while the Invesco Pharmaceuticals ETF tracks a rules-based index of 30 specialized companies engaged in drug research and distribution.

Beta measures price volatility relative to the S&P 500; beta is calculated from monthly returns over the available fund history (up to five years). The 1-year return represents total return over the trailing 12 months. Dividend yield is the trailing-12-month distribution yield.

The Simplify Health Care ETF is the more affordable option with a 0.51% expense ratio, compared to the 0.58% charged by the Invesco Pharmaceuticals ETF. The yield difference is marginal, as PJP offers a slightly higher trailing-12-month payout than PINK.

The Simplify Health Care ETF is an actively managed fund that targets long-term capital appreciation by investing in healthcare sub-sectors like gene therapy, medical technology, and biotechnology. Managed by Michael Taylor, it maintains 58 holdings and allocates about 91% of its portfolio to healthcare stocks, with small exposures to consumer cyclicals and industrials. Its largest positions include Eli Lilly and Co. (LLY +1.26%) at 8.15%, Humana (HUM +1.42%) at 8.08%, and Arcutis Biotherapeutics (ARQT +2.65%) at 6.60%. The fund was launched in 2021 and carries a currency hedge quirk. It has paid $0.30 per share over the trailing 12 months, which on its recent ~$39 share price works out to a 0.8% yield.

The Invesco Pharmaceuticals ETF focuses entirely on the U.S. pharmaceutical industry, providing concentrated exposure to companies involved in the research and manufacture of drugs. It currently holds 28 stocks and is classified as non-diversified. Its top holdings include AbbVie (ABBV +0.20%) at 5.87%, Gilead Sciences (GILD +0.08%) at 5.73%, and Pfizer (PFE +0.21%) at 5.58%. This fund was launched in 2005 and passively tracks a specific index rather than using active management. It has paid $1.13 per share over the trailing 12 months, which on its recent ~$123 share price works out to a 0.9% yield.

For more guidance on ETF investing, check out the full guide at this link.

Investors interested in healthcare exposure have very different choices in the Invesco Pharmaceuticals ETF (PJP) and Simplify Health Care ETF (PINK). For starters, the latter is actively-managed while PJP is a passive fund. Several additional factors may impact your decision to choose between these ETFs.

PINK donates its profits to the Susan G. Komen Foundation in the fight against breast cancer, so your investment contributes to this cause. It seeks to invest broadly across the healthcare sector without regard to company weightings in industry indices. Its expense ratio is lower, saving you money, and it is more diversified than PJP. However, its one-year and five-year returns are not as strong, and the fund has only existed since 2021, providing limited visibility into its performance over various macroeconomic cycles.

PJP seeks to replicate the performance of the Dynamic Pharmaceutical Intellidex Index, so its holdings reflect that. It's been around since 2005, providing over 20 years of history to help you gauge long-term returns. It's the ideal fund if you want exposure to domestic drugmakers and major pharmaceutical catalysts such as the GLP-1 weight-loss drugs. Yet that same focus can be a drawback if the pharma industry experiences a downturn.

#2939. Nvidia Stock Is Down About a Third From Its October Peak. Here's My Estimate for 2030.

The AI chipmaker's revenue more than doubled last quarter. How much of this growth could the stock keep by the end of 2030?

Shares of Nvidia (NVDA -2.01%) ended Oct. 6 at $239.24, a record close. As I write this, they trade near $159, about a third lower.

Some of the fall came this past week. Shares slid 4% on Nov. 12, when Nvidia's board announced Jensen Huang's resignation as CEO. Colette Kress, the company's CFO, is filling in as interim CEO while the board looks for a permanent one.

But the business is growing faster than it was a year ago. And management has already given a rough outlook for its next fiscal year.

With Nvidia's own outlook and some cautious assumptions beyond it, I estimate the stock could trade near $240 at the end of 2030 -- close to where it peaked in October. That'd be about 50% over today's price, or a return of around 10% a year. (This estimate comes before Nvidia's fiscal third-quarter results, due Tuesday, Nov. 17.)

Showing how strong demand remains, Nvidia's revenue growth has sped up for four straight quarters. Sales climbed 62% year over year in fiscal 2026's third quarter, then 73% and 85%. And in the fiscal second quarter of 2027 (the three months ended July 26, 2026), revenue more than doubled, up 106% to $96.2 billion.

Nvidia's fiscal Q3 forecast of $108 billion would put sales for the first nine months of the year around $286 billion. This alone is well over the $215.9 billion Nvidia took in for all of fiscal 2026.

If fourth-quarter sales simply matched that $108 billion, fiscal 2027 revenue would total around $394 billion, up 82%.

On the August earnings call, Kress said management's preliminary expectation is for fiscal 2028 revenue to climb about 70%. That year ends in January 2028. She added that customers' forecasts suggest even faster growth, so the 70% shows what Nvidia can supply, not what buyers want.

Applied to my fiscal 2027 number, that means around $670 billion in revenue.

But earnings likely won't keep up. Memory costs have risen, and Kress expects gross margin to drop to a low of 71% to 72% in the fiscal fourth quarter, down from 75% in fiscal Q2.

After fiscal 2028, I've assumed Nvidia's revenue grows just 5% a year, mainly because of what Kress said about supply.

"[W]e expect supply to remain a bottleneck at least through the end of fiscal year 2028," she said.

When chip supply catches up to orders, Nvidia might lose some of its power to set prices. And growing 5% a year from there brings revenue to around $775 billion in fiscal 2031, which ends in January 2031 and closely matches calendar 2030.

Also, Nvidia kept about 56% of its fiscal Q2 revenue as non-GAAP (adjusted) net income. I used 45% for fiscal 2031, a big step down that allows for lower prices and stiffer competition. This comes out to about $349 billion in profit.

Buybacks help, though slowly. Nvidia's diluted share count shrank about 1% over the last year to roughly 24.3 billion, even with $20 billion in repurchases in fiscal Q2 alone. Keeping this pace puts me at about 23.3 billion shares, or earnings per share near $15.

Now, the stock costs around 20 times earnings, which is arguably cheap for a business whose sales just doubled. But my estimate has Nvidia growing in single digits by 2030, so I assumed investors pay less: 16 times earnings. At $15 a share, the stock would be worth around $240. Also, the quarterly dividend of $0.25 a share adds a little under 1% a year.

Of course, the biggest risk to this number is the price-to-earnings multiple investors are willing to pay, and the leadership change matters here. The board said it'll consider internal and external candidates for the permanent job. Until it names one, investors could hesitate to pay up for Nvidia's profits.

At 12 times earnings, the same $15 comes to about $180 -- only around 3% a year from here.

Is around 10% a year enough to make the stock worth buying? I think so. Climbing back to $240 doesn't require Nvidia to keep growing fast past next year. It mainly requires the 70% outlook to hold up and investors to pay a normal price for the earnings that follow.

I'd consider buying stock near $159, but I'd keep my position small as the CEO search plays out. Of course, it's just an estimate.

#2933. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 stocks for the long haul

Global stock markets have been volatile as investors assess high bond yields and continued tensions between the U.S. and Iran. There are concerns about the impact of uncertainty in the Middle East on oil prices and consequently on inflation.

Investors who prefer to look beyond short-term noise and buy stocks with attractive long-term growth potential can follow the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts. The ratings of these experts are backed by in-depth research of a company's financials and thorough analysis of the factors affecting its business performance.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Semiconductor giant Nvidia (NVDA) reported stellar fiscal second-quarter results and reassured investors about continued strength in AI-driven demand for its chips. Moreover, Nvidia's FY28 revenue growth outlook of 70% was well above the Street's expectations.

Reacting to the Q2 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reiterated a buy rating on NVDA and increased his price target to $300 from $288. "This is our Top Pick in the semis group, with a compelling product cycle, exceptional growth and valuation below peers," said the 5-star analyst.

Nvidia delivered impressive quarterly results despite supply constraints, Moore said, but he believes that the FY28 revenue and gross margin commentary mattered more, with a favorable outlook for both measures.

Moore said Nvidia's 70% revenue growth guidance for FY28 was above his own estimate of 52% and the consensus of about 40%. The top-line growth estimate was a "remarkable figure" given supply issues and Moore said he expects Nvidia to continue to address bottlenecks to deliver rapid growth.

Among other takeaways, the analyst said performance metrics for Vera Rubin chips were strong, with Nvidia emphasizing a 30x higher throughput per megawatt and 35x lower token cost compared with Grace Blackwell Ultra.

Moore ranks No. 159 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 24.60%. See Nvidia Statistics on TipRanks.

Ride-hailing and delivery platform Uber Technologies (UBER) was recommended by BMO Capital analyst Brian Pitz, who discussed the company's opportunity in the autonomous vehicle (AV) market in a recent research note that reiterated a buy rating and price target of $119.

The 5-star analyst highlighted Uber's transition into a diversified marketplace, with investors now focusing on its AV growth prospects. The key question is how much value can Uber generate as AVs become commercialized, Pitz asked.

"We continue to believe that Uber's AV strategy is evolving and will become a significant driver of revenue and profitability," the analyst said.

Importantly, Pitz believes that Uber can establish itself as a preferred mobility platform for AV makers, supported by its growing AV infrastructure capabilities and an expanding partner base. This would enable Uber to win a significant share of value as the AV market is commercialized.

Additionally, Pitz noted Uber's enhanced position in the AV value chain, with the company shifting from serving as a distribution channel for robotaxis to becoming a broader platform for AV mobility. The analyst discussed Uber's efforts to capture growth opportunities in the AV market, including higher capital spending, expansion into new markets and building partnerships beyond Alphabet's Waymo.

Pitz ranks No. 574 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of about 12.20%. See Uber Technologies Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Semiconductor company Marvell Technology (MRVL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the second quarter of FY27. But the company's increased FY28 guidance fell short of investors' high expectations.

In reaction to the Q2 FY27 results, KeyBanc analyst John Vinh reaffirmed a buy rating on Marvell Technology, with a price target of $400. The analyst noted that Marvell's results and outlook were driven by strength in its data center business. The Q2 FY27 data center business grew 46% year-over-year, to $2.17 billion, far above KeyBanc's estimate of $2.09 billion.

Management "highlighted accelerating AI networking demand across scale-out, scale-across and scale-up architectures, supported by strong 800G optical DSP demand, rapid 1.6T ramp, broader 51.2T switch deployments and higher TIA/driver demand," Vinh said.

Consequently, Marvell raised its data center revenue guidance, now expecting 60% growth in FY27 and more than 60% in FY28.

Regarding the recently announced $120 billion warrant arrangement with Google, Vinh said management argued that programs tied to the warrant are already priced into the custom outlook through FY28. Meanwhile, new programs and those in execution are expected to drive significant upside to the previous FY29 custom XPU revenue estimate of over $10 billion, Vinh said, citing management.

Vinh ranks No. 123 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 28.50%. See Marvell Technology Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2929. Amazon vs. Target: Which Stock Is the Better Portfolio Addition?

Amazon's cloud and retail empire faces off against Target's curated shelves, but the valuation gap may settle the debate before the strategies do.

The competition between Amazon (AMZN -0.29%) and Target (TGT +0.34%) highlights two distinct paths for investors. Investing in Amazon is about seeking greater stock price appreciation in the future, while owning Target is more about getting paid today through collecting dividends.

Amazon leverages a massive logistics network and high-margin cloud services to drive growth, while Target focuses on providing a differentiated, multi-category shopping experience. These companies often compete for the same household spending, yet they offer very different financial profiles. Further choosing between them requires understanding their unique strengths and the specific risks they face in an evolving market.

Amazon operates a sprawling ecosystem that includes an online store, third-party seller services, and the high-growth AWS cloud computing division. The company serves a diverse range of customers including individual consumers, developers, and government entities through its massive logistics network. By maintaining strategic relationships with millions of vendors, it ensures a constant flow of goods and services across North America and international markets.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached nearly $797.5 billion, representing growth of approximately 15.4%. Its net margin was approximately 16.9% as the company generated a net income of roughly $134.8 billion during this same period. This performance highlights the significant scale of its operations as it continues to expand its reach in digital services and advertising.

Target operates as a general merchandise retailer, emphasizing a curated selection of essentials and trend-forward products across nearly 2,000 stores. It thrives by fostering guest relationships and maintaining partnerships with high-profile brands like Apple (AAPL +0.39%), Starbucks (SBUX -0.18%), and Ulta Beauty (ULTA +1.49%). This strategy is central to its position among retail stocks as it successfully integrates physical locations with digital delivery options.

In the 12 months ended Aug. 1, 2026, revenue was roughly $107.7 billion, showing growth of approximately 2% compared to the prior twelve-month period. Target maintained a net margin of roughly 4.1% and reported a net income of nearly $4.4 billion for this timeframe. While its growth pace is slower than its tech-heavy rival, the retailer remains a vital part of the American consumer landscape.

Target faces risks from intense competition and the need to differentiate its guest experience through pricing and assortment. The company is vulnerable to inventory management challenges, including exposure to theft and organized retail crime that can impact profitability. Additionally, it remains highly dependent on U.S. macroeconomic conditions and a global supply chain that is susceptible to geopolitical instability and trade policy changes.

Target is cheaper based on its lower forward price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio and price-to-sales (P/S) ratio, which measures market cap against sales over the past twelve months.

Although both serve a retail base, Amazon is much more aligned as a technology company than Target is. In addition, what each stock offers is also notably different.

Amazon may not offer the explosive stock price appreciation of an up-and-coming quantum computing stock. But owning it still depends on what it can do for investors, more so in the future than today. One example is that Amazon reported in its 2026 third-quarter earnings report that 12-month free cash flow turned from a $14.8 billion inflow to a $42.4 billion outflow, thanks largely to spending more on building out artificial intelligence infrastructure. And the important takeaway there is that, with aggressive spending today, Amazon is asking its shareholders to judge its future path more than today's free cash flow. Broadly, owning Amazon is about how big its business could be in five to 10 years.

In comparison, owning Target is more about cash generation today and returning it to shareholders, with its dividend currently yielding 2.8%. Through the first six months of 2026, operating cash flow was $4.5 billion for Target, up from the $2.4 billion it reported in the prior-year period. That gives it plenty of flexibility to invest further in its stores, pay down debt, or have the money flow to dividend payouts. In step with some of the improvements Target is seeing in its financials, the stock price has climbed 65% this year, compared to a 10.4% return for the S&P 500 over the same period. While stock price appreciation typically isn't the main consideration for an income stock, it's helpful to have, because you don't want a decreasing stock price cutting into the returns from those dividend payouts.

Between the two, Amazon's story is more about what it can build itself into, while Target is about improving its current business and passing on what it makes to shareholders through dividend payouts.

#2925. Michael Burry's Puts on the Nasdaq-100 Run to April 2027. I'm Not Following Him.

The Big Short investor has bet almost all his short book on a tech top within months. Should Nasdaq-100 fund holders hedge too?

Michael Burry is betting that one of the market's best-known tech indexes has further to fall. In an Oct. 11 post on his Substack, the investor famous for The Big Short said almost all his bets against the market were now put options on two things.

One is the Nasdaq-100 index, with contracts expiring in April 2027. The other is the VanEck Semiconductor ETF (SMH -2.99%), with contracts expiring in June 2027. A put gains value as the asset under it drops.

The index has gone his way since then. The Invesco QQQ Trust (QQQ -1.20%), the exchange-traded fund that tracks the Nasdaq-100, trades around $680 as I write this, about 10% under the peak it reached on Oct. 6. And on Nov. 5 alone, the Nasdaq-100 sank 6.4%.

Should investors who own the fund protect themselves as Burry has? I don't think so, and my reasons have more to do with his calendar than with his take on tech stocks.

Michael Burry. Image source: Getty Images.

Burry has argued that companies are borrowing and spending too much to build data centers for AI. And in October, he said he thought the market might be anywhere from zero to nine months away from a major top. Starting from his October post, that window runs into July 2027 -- past the April expiration of his Nasdaq-100 puts and the June expiration of his chip-fund puts.

That gap matters, because a put that expires before the drop comes can expire worthless. In September, Burry closed out his December 2026 puts on Nvidia to avoid losing more of their value to time decay (the steady drain an option suffers as expiration nears).

He's also been trading around the bet. On Nov. 5, the chip fund dropped 11%, and Burry sold 25% of his puts on it. He said the puts he kept were still a big position and that he was still bearish. Later, on Nov. 12, he said he'd doubled his Nasdaq-100 put position and added back to his chip-fund puts.

In other words, Burry isn't just holding insurance. He's making a string of timing calls.

Of course, Burry's two bets overlap more than it may seem. The 10 largest holdings in QQQ make up around 46% of its assets. And four chipmakers -- Nvidia, Advanced Micro Devices, Micron Technology, and Broadcom -- account for about 18% by themselves.

A chip sell-off like the one on Nov. 5 can hit the fund hard. But a falling price isn't the same as falling profits.

Showing how the fund's biggest companies are still growing, Apple (AAPL +0.39%), its largest holding, said its earnings per share rose 14% year over year in its fiscal 2026 fourth quarter (the three months ending Sept. 26), with a slight boost from tariff refunds. Microsoft (MSFT +0.17%), its third-largest holding, grew earnings per share on a non-GAAP (adjusted) basis by 18% year over year in its fiscal 2027 first quarter (the three months ending Sept. 30). That adjusted number leaves out gains and losses on the software giant's OpenAI stake. Growth is slowing at both companies, though. Apple's earnings per share had gained 29% in its fiscal third quarter. And Microsoft's adjusted earnings per share had risen 23% in the quarter before and 22% for fiscal 2026, so 18% was a step down.

But earnings of the fund's two biggest non-chip companies are growing at double-digit rates while its price has fallen around 10% from the Oct. 6 peak. In other words, QQQ's valuation is arguably lower relative to what its largest companies earn than it was a bit over a month ago.

For a long-term holder, buying puts like Burry's means paying for insurance that runs out. If the index holds up through April, the puts might expire worthless, and their cost comes right out of the holder's returns.

And if a big fall does arrive, the holder still has to choose when to sell the puts. That's the same sort of timing call Burry has been making.

Someone planning to hold a Nasdaq-100 fund for the next decade probably doesn't need to be right about April 2027.

In the end, Burry might be right that tech stocks have further to fall, and a day like Nov. 5 could come again before his puts expire. But his puts are a bet on timing, while a fund holder's result mainly depends on whether the companies in the fund keep growing their earnings. Right now, the biggest ones are.

For investors who already own a Nasdaq-100 fund, I'd keep holding it and skip the puts, even if that means riding out a few more tough days.

#2918. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#2914. Fred VanVleet is Rockets unsung hero

The point guard position has undergone a radical transformation over the last decade and a half or so. Like any seismic change, it's hard to pinpoint a precise catalyst.

When Derrick Rose became the youngest MVP in NBA history in 2011, that was a watershed moment. His 7.7 assists per game were point guard-y enough, but the 25.1 points per game would have imposed a "shooting guard" label on him at any other point in league history. Rose was the new meta.

Houston Rockets fans know the change intimately. Firstly, Mike D'Antoni's decision to run the offense almost exclusively through James Harden was another critical juncture. Fast forward, and we're arguing ad nauseam about whether Kevin "I am obviously not a point guard" Porter Jr. is a point guard.

Here's what makes VanVleet postmodern: He's a throwback, in a sense. Ball security is his superpower. VanVleet facilitates in a low-risk, medium-reward way.

Yet, he's also modern. VanVleet launches pull-up threes that would make Mark Jackson blush. It's the only means he really has of bending defenses, and modern, starting-caliber point guards must bend defenses. At times, those shots can look a little bit absurd in the moment:

But in the bigger picture, they're part of what makes VanVleet so impactful.

Thanks, Brad. DataBallr has VanVleet's impact as even more positive, with a +14.1 On/Off split. I'm not sure why different outlets calculate on/off stats differently, but what matters is that the stats consistently demonstrate that VanVleet is a winner.

VanVleet's True Shooting % (TS%) is buoyed by his combined three-point volume and efficiency. He's hitting 39.5% of his 7.4 threes per game. For this Rockets team, that's priceless.

Many have suggested that Alperen Sengun would thrive if paired with a more dynamic point guard. It would be easy to point out that (again, per DatalBallr) the duo is +13.8 when they share the floor, but there are solid normative explanations for their success as well.

Just as VanVleet is not Jamal Murray, Sengun is not Nikola Jokic. He's a less natural shooter, and he's not on Jokic's level as a passer. That's not a knock: Sengun is a top-ten (and closer to five) passing big man in NBA history, but Jokic is a top-ten (and closer to five, and really closer to two or three) passer in NBA history.

But Sengun is perhaps a better ball-handler (it feels ethically necessary to note that he's a much better defender here, but it's not relevant here). That ball-handling ability makes him one of the best isolation bigs in the NBA, but some of Sengun's pick-and-roll finishing looks a lot like isolation basketball too. I've said this before, but Sengun and VanVleet run the pick-and-roll like Stockton and Malone. Often, the purpose of the set is largely to advance Sengun's position by a few inches before he starts cooking.

It's a job that, say, Kyrie Irving is overqualified for. That's not to say that outright that the Rockets shouldn't trade for him (for example, although it feels like he's the only star guard they're at all likely to acquire). There's a part of me that thinks the 2026-27 team is our best window, and more pure offensive firepower is what we need.

There's another (and ultimately, I think, stronger) part of me that thinks VanVleet's willingness and ability to let Sengun cook is what's best for his continuing development. The stats strongly suggest that VanVleet makes Sengun better, and the Rockets, in general, a better team:

That's as close to an objective truth as one could possibly find.

#2904. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Federal Register notice: Modifications to the Capital Plan Rule and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement

Federal Register notice: Request for Comment on Model Changes for the Boardâs 2027 Supervisory Stress Test

#2901. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#2891. Dallas Mavericks (7-6) at Boston Celtics (10-2) Game #13 11/15/26

Dallas Mavericks (7-6) at Boston Celtics (10-2)Sunday, November 15, 20266:00 PM ESTRegular Season Game #13 Home Game #7TV: NBCSB, KFAA29, NBA League PassRadio: 98.5 Sports Hub, 105.3 The Fan, Sirius XMTD Garden

The Celtics return home from a 4-0 road trip to take on the Dallas Mavericks. This is the first of 2 meetings between these 2 teams this season. They will play in Dallas on November 30. The Celtics won the series 2-0 last season. They are 49-41 overall all time against the Mavericks and they are 28-17 all time in games played in Boston.

Dallas is playing in the second of back to back games, having lost at Cleveland on Saturday. The Celtics are playing in the first of back to back games. They will host Orlando on Monday. This is already their 4th back to back set. They are 3-0 in the first of back to back games so far. The Celtics have 2 more back to back sets left in the month of November.

The Celtics are 1st in the East, half a game ahead of 2nd place Philadelphia and 1.5 games ahead of 3rd place Detroit. They are 5-0 at home and 9-1 in their last 10 games. This is their first game against a Western Conference team. They have won their last 5 games. The Mavericks are 10th in the West. They are 2-4 on the road and 6-4 in their last 10 games. They are 4-1 against Eastern Conference teams and have lost their last 2 games.

The Mavericks made several moves in the off season, starting with hiring Masai Ujiri to replace Nico Harrison, who sealed his fate when he traded Luka Doncic for Anthony Davis. They also replaced Jason Kidd with Dusty May as coach. They added Zaccharie Risacher, Santi Aldama, Marcus Sasser, and Tarik Biberovic. They drafted Morez Johnson with the 9th pick in the draft and Sertio DeLarrea with the 25th pick. They Klay Thompson, Khris Middleton, Ryan Nembhard, Marvin Bagley III, and Dwight Powell.

After this game, the Celtics will host Orlando before a West coast trip that takes them through Denver, Sacramento, Golden State, and Washington. They then play one game at home against Atlanta before playing at San Antonio and Dallas. Then it's home against Charlotte before a game at Brooklyn. Next they have a 7 game home stand against Washington twice, Utah, Detroit, Portland, Miami and Cleveland.

After this game, Dallas will return home to host Golden State and the LA Clippers before a road trip through Utah, Denver, and Phoenix. Then they will host Boston, Orlando and Milwaukee before playing at Brooklyn and Philadelphia. Then it's one game at home against Chicago before games at Golden State and Sacramento.

Predicting the Celtics starting lineup for any game is tough. The Celtics have been changing the 5th starter on a regular basis and there is no telling who they will start from game to game. I've listed the most used starting lineup for them but it may change. I personally would like to see Baylor Scheierman start in place of Jordan Walsh. For the Mavericks, I've listed their regular starting lineup of Irving, Flagg, Martin, Washington and Gafford, although they may rest a player or two on the second night of back to back games.

At this time, the Celtics have no one on the injury report, but it's possible they may rest a player due to playing in back to back games. The Mavericks have 8 players on the injury list at this time. Tarik Biberrovic is out with right heel bursitis. Sergio DeLarrea is out with a right knee sprain. Dereck Lively II is out due to foot surgery. Max Christie is doubtful due to an ankle sprain. Santi Aldama is questionable with an ankle injury. Daniel Gafford is probable with a shoulder injury. Kyrie Irving is questionable due to knee injury management. If Kyrie is out for injury management, I would guess that Marcus Sasser gets the start in his place.

Probable Starting Matchups PG: Derrick White vs Kyrie Irving

Celtics ReservesChris Cenac, JrMike Conley, JrLuka GarzaHugo GonzalezRon Harper, JrSam HauserPayton PritchardMitchell RobinsonBaylor Scheierman

Two Way PlayersDevin CarterDillon MitchellAmari Williams

Mavericks ReservesSanti AldamaMoussa CisseMorez Johnson, JrNaji MarshallZaccharie RisacherMarcus Sasser

2-Way PlayersWalter Clayton, JrJett HowardTobi Lawal

Injuries/OutTarik Biberovic (heel) outSergio Delarrea (knee) outDereck Lively II (foot) outMax Christie (ankle) questionableSanti Aldama (ankle) questionableKyrie Irving (knee) questionable Max Christie (ankle) doubtfulDaniel Gafford (shoulder) probable

Key Matchups Jordan Walsh vs Cooper FlaggFlagg is playing very well to start his sophomore season. He is averaging 24.6 points, 6.9 rebounds, 6.5 assists and 2.2 steals per game. He is shooting 49.5% from the field and 30.0% from beyond the arc. The Celtics need to defend him well and avoid giving him open shots. The Celtics also have to be aware of his defense as he is very good at deflecting the ball and getting steals.

Derrick White vs Kyrie IrvingAfter missing all of last season, Kyrie Irving is back and playing very well. He is averaging 21.6 points, 3.2 rebounds,4.3 assists and 1.1 steals per game. He is shooting 47.9% from the field and 39.0% from beyond the arc. He has long carried a grudge against the Celtics, even going so far as to stomp on the logo after a game at the Garden and so always gives his best effort to try to beat them. He is questionable due to the back to back and for injury management and so if he can't start, I'm guessing that Marcus Sasser gets the start in his place.

Honorable MentionJayson Tatum vs PJ Washington, JrWashington is averaging 14.3 points, 7.3 rebounds, and 1.1 assists per game. He is shooting 41.8% from the field and 35.1% from beyond the arc. The Celtics need to keep him out of the paint and off the boards. They also need to avoid leaving him open on the perimeter.

Keys to the GameDefense – Defense is always the number one key to winning games. There is no truer statement than "Defense wins Championships." The Mavs have an offensive rating of 112.5 (16th) while the Celtics have an offensive rating of 117.4 (6th). The Mavs are 12th with a defensive rating of 111.7 while the Celtics are 2nd with a defensive rating of 105.6. When the Celtics struggle on offense, as they sometimes do, they have to be able to stop the other team from scoring. The Celtics especially need to defend the paint as the Mavericks average 48.3 points in the paint per game. The Celtics have to continue to make defense their identity.

Rebound – Second to defense, rebounding is the key to winning. One of the few times I have agreed with Pat Riley was when he said "No rebounds, no rings." The Celtics are averaging 44.8 rebounds per game (8th) while the Mavs are averaging 44.0 rebounds per game (15th). The Mavs are 6th with 16.5 second chance points per game. The Celtics need to put out extra effort on the boards to keep the Mavs from getting extra possessions and second chance points and to give the same to themselves.

Be Aggressive – The Celtics must be the more aggressive team. They have to be more aggressive in going to the basket, in diving for loose balls, and on defense. They have to aggressively crash the boards. They have to be the team that wants to win the game more. They absolutely can't let the Mavericks play harder than them if they want to win this game. Hard work beats talent when talent doesn?t work hard and when talent works hard, they are very hard to beat.

Move the Ball Carefully – The Celtics are at their best when the ball moves and they make the extra pass. When the ball sticks and players try to do too much, the Celtics struggle. But they have to make careful passes and not get sloppy because the Mavericks average 18.4 points off turnovers and 17.2 fast break points per game. The Celtics need to be careful with their passes and ball handling because if they aren't the Mavericks will make them pay.

X-FactorsAt Home and Rest – The Mavericks will be on the road and playing on the second night of back to back games. They will have the distractions of travel and playing in front of a mostly hostile crowd,although I expect a lot of cheers for hometown Cooper Flagg. Fatigue could be a factor for the Mavs while the Celtics have had 3 days off and should be well rested. The Celtics need to get motivation from the home crowd and defend their home court with energy and effort.

Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game. Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game. They did a good job of overcoming a 28-5 free throw disadvantage against the 76ers. The Celtics also have to be aware of the new points of emphasis for this season and try to avoid flopping, off‑arm push‑offs, reckless closeouts, delays before free throws, and illegal screens. They have been calling all of these and so the Celtics need to make a conscious attempt to avoid them.

#2889. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

Recently, BMO Capital analyst Devin Dodge reaffirmed a buy rating on Brookfield Infrastructure with a price target of $47. The analyst cited improved visibility into BIP's growth and views its corporate simplification as a potential catalyst.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

Dodge ranks No. 476 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 68% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.5%. See Brookfield Infrastructure Partners KPIs on TipRanks.

#2885. Titans vs. Cowboys odds: Opening lines for Week 11 matchup

For the 100th straight week*, the Tennessee Titans have opened as underdogs. This week, they are a 7.5-point underdog at the Dallas Cowboys. This could be a really fun game to watch. Neither team is good on defense. Both teams, dare I say it, can put up points on offense. I wouldn't touch the spread in this one, but the over 52.5 looks like a pretty good bet.

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2874. Embiid-less Sixers get just enough from center position in win vs. Pacers

It's been awhile since we saw a 1 p.m. win on a Sunday in this city. The Sixers knocked off the Indiana Pacers 131-124 Sunday Afternoon.

Jaylen Brown powered the Sixers with 31 points shooting 12-of-18 from the floor along with three rebounds. Tyrese Maxey quietly put up 27 points and six assists shooting 9-of-19 from the field.

LeBron James had a tremendous day sharing the rock as his 11 assists moved him up to third in the league's all-time assist leaders. He also had 15 points and six rebounds on 5-of-8 shooting. VJ Edgecombe bounced back from a scoreless first half to end with 11 points, four rebounds, and six assists shooting 5-of-11 from the floor. Pascal Siakam led the Pacers with 31 as well.

Joel Embiid was out with plantar fascia management in his right foot, along with Adem Bona and Dominick Barlow. The Pacers were missing Tyrese Haliburton, Andrew Nembhard and Ben Sheppard.

The Sixers moved the ball very well on their opening possession of the game as Maxey got wide open on a cut, but he smoked the bunny. It looked like he might not have corralled the bounce pass from LeBron. Maxey did get the Sixers on the board running quick off the ensuing Pacers miss. Brown knocked down two of his first three jumpers as the two of them accounted for Philly's first 15 points of the game.

A lot of what Maxey was able to get early was set up by LeBron, who was zipping the ball all around the perimeter early. Maxey took full advantage and had a lot of space to get his first two three-pointers off. Maxey found James up ahead for a transition bucket for James' first points of the day.

Brown had a really good jump shooting quarter, putting up 11 in the first. He didn't seem to have a hard time getting to his pull-up spots, and knocked them down whether there was a hand in his face or not. Meanwhile on the other end, all five of the Pacers' starters scored within the first six minutes of the game and started the game 11-of-19 from the floor. They were already taking advantage of the small Sixers team available with nine early second-chance points on four offensive rebounds.

Kelly Oubre Jr. was playing in Philly for the first time since signing with the Pacers, and got off to a good start. He scored seven in the first, beating his old team on a couple of cuts. Another old friend, T.J. McConnell, hit a few of his classic short pull-up jumpers. The Sixers trailed by six after the Pacers shot 62% from the field in the first.

A quick rest on the bench didn't cool Brown down as he nailed two more jumpers to star the second. After getting some minutes in Delaware over the weekend, Labaron Philon Jr. was back in the rotation, checking in early in the second, even if it was because Anfernee Simons picked up two quick fouls. The first time he got into an action was LeBron screening and rolling for him, but the play was quickly whistled dead when Quentin Jackson got into it with James. A couple possessions later James hit Philon on the wing for a three.

Regardless of whether the Sixers had Jabari Walker on the floor or a less traditional center option, they had trouble giving the Pacers any resistance at the rim. Fourteen of Indy's first 16 points in the second came from the paint or getting fouled in the paint. With that shot diet it wasn't a shock they continued to hover over 60% from the field.

Brown continued to find ways to put the ball in the basket, and the Sixers needed every bit of it to keep pace. There was even a sequence where he grabbed a rebound from the floor despite getting hit in the face earlier in the possession.

After a couple of fast breaks Maxey simply created with his speed. James threw a brilliant bounce pass down the baseline to Brown for another fast break that capped off a 16-4 Sixer run. Coming out of a Pacer timeout, Oubre drew a foul on a Brown push-off, something Oubre really exaggerated in celebration. Aaron Nesmith scored a basket out of that, but Oubre missed the corner three the Pacers got out of a Maxey turnover their next trip down the floor.

LeBron took that rebound and got up the floor quickly for a dunk attempt before the half but Oubre fouled him hard to avoid that. James hit the deck hard and stayed down for a bit, but eventually got up and knocked down two free throws to put the Sixers up by one at the break. Whether it was related or not, James and Maxey had a lengthy discussion with Tyrese Haliburton before heading back to the locker room.

Going scoreless in the first half, Edgecombe just could not buy a bucket. He missed another open corner three early in the third, but did start to produce. He nabbed a steal to start the quarter and found Maxey for a three, then finally got a couple of baskets himself going to the basket. He tried his damndest to put Ivica Zubac on a poster, but Zubac got over with enough time to meet Edgecombe with a hard foul.

A center with a clear height advantage like Zubac was always going to give Walker trouble defensively, but he was putting in another good shift of hardwork. On top of pulling down four defensive rebounds and two on the offensive end, Walker had drilled three threes which is always a nice help to the offense.

With that being said, Nick Nurse decided to give Tacko Fall his frst real minutes when Walker went to the bench. Fall rebounded Edgecombe's missed free throw, which once again sent the crowd into a wild frenzy. They also ate up every bit of the screen he set for LeBron as James scored eight straight for the Sixers.

There were some rusty moments like a foul and a defensive three-second violation, but the Fall minutes definitely could have gone worse defensively. Multiple shots at the rim were altered by him being there, including a layup attempt that went out of bounds over the backboard.

Their defense to close the quarter looked more comfortable. Dean Wade made a great play to stop a fastbreak, and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope fought for a loose ball that led to him earning a pair of free throws. Maxey fell into a rhythm again on the other end, hitting a floater and another stepback three. Simons hit his second quarter-ending buzzer-beater in as many games, giving the Sixers a 10-point lead after the third.

After such a strong first half Brown had to sit most of the third quarter when he quickly picked up his third foul. That long rest didn't slow his momentum down though as he hit a pull-up to start the quarter before trailing LeBron to hit an open three off the catch.

A few possessions later Edgecombe made a nice read to open up Wade in the corner, setting the Sixers up to good position to start running away with the game. A three from Josh Huff followed by two consecutive and-1s by Siakam made sure that this game was far from over. The Sixers responded with a possession of great ball movement that, as the tweet below points out, was entirely orchestrated by LeBron, and led to another Wade three. However Siakam answered again by getting right to the basket and would pick up another and-1 two possessions later.

The Sixers' offense did stagnate a bit, especially when LeBron went to the bench. They were lucky they weren't punished more during their two minutes scoreless stretch. The only time they stopped Siakam in this stretch was when he pulled up for a jumper. Brown was finally able to snap that with a midrange but the Pacers were still able to chop the lead to four. KCP hit a big three to push that lead back up as LeBron was waiting to check back in.

James threw another dime to a baseline cutter on his first possession back in the game, and Wade was able to draw a foul as he finished the dunk, putting the Sixers back up by double-digits with just over two to go. After Brown split a pair of free throws, Siakam got another and-1. He missed the free throw, and after each team committed an offensive foul, that pretty much put the Pacers in fouling territory from there on out.

#2872. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.
