#2763. How Rockstar's 'GTA VI' release could reshape gaming, streaming and even paid time off

It's been 13 years since the last installment of Grand Theft Auto, and fans are getting ready for what analysts say could be the biggest video game launch of all time.

The latest game in the franchise, "Grand Theft Auto VI," is set to launch on Nov. 19, after Take-Two Interactive's Rockstar Games delayed its launch twice. Roth analyst Eric Handler estimated the game maker spent more than $1 billion developing the game.

Sales figures could end up breaking records. Sensor Tower estimated over 12 million preorders through Nov. 13, and Morgan Stanley forecast the game could sell 40 million copies by March 2027.

Beyond launch week, the Grand Theft Auto, or GTA, franchise has a track record of lasting engagement over many years. Even 13 years after launch, "Grand Theft Auto V" is one of the top five most viewed games on Twitch this year.

In anticipation of the game's launch, investors have been taking note: Take-two's stock has more than doubled since the beginning of 2023.

The release is seen as not only a monumental moment for developer Rockstar Games and parent company Take-Two, but also a massive opportunity for the entire gaming industry.

Take-Two CEO Strauss Zelnick addressed demand in a recent "Squawk Box" interview, saying, "We tend to be pretty judicious when we talk about this sort of thing, but our expectations were high and they are being vastly exceeded so far."

A creator who goes by the name Deansocool, who has 375,000 followers on Twitch and posts content to YouTube and other social platforms as well, said "GTA VI" will enable creators to grow their audience and provide a massive opportunity for newer streamers to make a name for themselves.

"This game is going to make a lot of millionaires in 2027. A lot of creators gotta understand this is it. This is that golden ticket. This is like a hundred times bigger than Fortnite," he said in an interview with CNBC.

"It goes beyond this launch moment into this sustaining opportunity for them to grow, build, engage and retain their audience and ultimately support themselves by making money," said Twitch Chief Product Officer Mike Minton.

The release of the latest Grand Theft Auto title could draw so much attention that its impact will be felt beyond just the world of gaming.

BambooHR, a workplace time-off platform, reported that paid time off requests for the "GTA VI" release date are nearly 13% higher than equivalent days in 2024 and 2025. It also noted that nearly one in five workers are planning to take time off for the launch.

PwC issued a report examining all the different sectors of the economy that could benefit from the game's launch — or be hurt by it. The firm projected that food delivery companies could see a surge from hunkered-down gamers, and Miami tourism could benefit. PwC noted that foreign visitors to Seoul's Bukchon Hanok Village, which inspired a location in the hit Netflix movie KPop Demon Hunters, increased 118% in the four weeks following the movie's debut.

Music streaming also looks set to get a boost. Tom Petty's "Love Is a Long Road," which appeared in the first "GTA VI" trailer in December 2023, reportedly saw Spotify streams jump by more than 37,000%.

On the downside, the game could distract from movies and streaming entertainment.

"How are people spending Thanksgiving? There's a lot of sports on TV, but you've also got a pretty solid group of your demographics that are going to be focused on playing 'GTA VI,'" said Bart Spiegel, who leads PwC's Global Entertainment & Media sector. "You're going to start to pull eyeballs away from video streaming platforms.

"I think you're going to hear a lot of analyst calls in the first quarter of 2027 saying, 'Hey, listen, some of our numbers were down because of the release of this game,'" Spiegel added.

Rockstar Games declined CNBC's interview request. Watch the video above to learn more.

#2762. The Colts playing Daniel Jones is indefensible

Why? Just why? Why is Daniel Jones out there for the Indianapolis Colts? It literally doesn't get any worse, so maybe that's the reason. Maybe it's the $50 million Chris Ballard gave him this year. Maybe he has something on Shane Steichen? I don't know what it is. All I know is Jones shouldn't see the field again this year.

Jones started his career with the Colts in the most incredible fashion. It is ending that way too. Incredibly. It is incredible how he is playing right now. A 1.7 QBR is the worst of any starting quarterback this year. That's worse than Baker Mayfield completing zero passes. That's worse than Carson Wentz on Monday Night Football against the Bills. Both of those performances were dreadful statistically. Jones beat them both.

This wasn't against the Vikings' defense. This wasn't him trying to keep pace with the best offense in the league. This was against the 1-7 Dolphins who ran a quarterback out there for his first start. Jones threw two picks and had 108 yards. Not all picks are fully on the quarterback, but the one returned for a touchdown could have been thrown better. Everything this year for Jones could be better. Not that the options to replace him are glowing, but Shane Steichen sticking with him at this point is insanity. Nothing tells you he is the guy.

Wanting a change at quarterback isn't just whiny fandom. It is a legitimate concern. Daniel Jones was horrid against one of the worst teams in the league. Things don't get any easier than a home game against the Dolphins. Yes, talent has been depleted due to trades, but Jones has to be better. If he can't be, then he needs to be done. The Colts have to make the change.

#2761. 'We slept here': Flight delays linger but schedule is back on track for Monday

U.S. flights are set to run close to normal on Monday as airlines scramble to get back on track after the Federal Aviation Administration grounded departures nationwide for more than four hours on Friday.

Disruptions lingered on Sunday with thousands more flights canceled and delayed.

Close to 2,000 U.S. flights were canceled as of 4:45 p.m. ET and more than 7,500 were delayed, according to flight-tracking site FlightAware, down from the 6,697 cancellations and 9,000 delays on Saturday, and Friday's 9,201 cancellations and more than 7,400 delays.

American Airlines had the most cancellations on Sunday at 805 flights, about 13% of its schedule, followed by Delta Air Lines with 360 cancellations, or 7% of its schedule, according to aviation data firm Cirium. The airlines' home hubs of Dallas-Fort Worth International Airport and Atlanta Hartsfield-Jackson International Airport, respectively, saw some of the most severe delays in the U.S. Meanwhile, JetBlue Airways canceled close to a third of its flights.

"We slept here," said Beth Martin, who was trying to travel home to Birmingham, Alabama, from New York's LaGuardia Airport on Sunday at the end of her daughter's birthday trip. They were booked for Saturday night, and when delay announcements started rolling in from Delta Air Lines, they decided to make good use of the extra time: after strolling through Central Park, they went window shopping on Fifth Avenue.

But when the flight was canceled, the standby list for a backup flight looked grim, and their original Midtown hotel was charging about $700 for an additional night, the two slept at LaGuardia on Saturday. She said they found wheelchairs to sleep in outside security.

Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy on Saturday tied the issue to a router and work to update from copper wires, a system overseen by L3Harris Technologies, to a fiber-optic system. FAA administrator Bryan Bedford said the upgrades were part of "routine" maintenance to switch over to fiber optic.

"We are supporting the FAA in its investigation to understand the conditions that led to the Nov. 13 ground stop of our nation's air travel," L3Harris said in a statement Saturday afternoon.

Airlines waived change fees and fare differences and offered pilots and flight attendants extra pay to ease the delays.

Customers told CNBC they were booked on several flights before getting on board.

Justine Fritz, who works in sales for a consumer goods company, was in New York for a business event on Friday. She tried five different flights before getting on a flight to Houston, then flew four hours out of her way to get back to Minneapolis after her Saturday flight was canceled. Fritz said she had so many boarding passes that TSA wouldn't let her through security at first.

"It's been bananas," she told CNBC at the gate. Fritz said she made the best of it by shopping and getting a haircut, but she's still disappointed.

"I'm missing the first hour of the Vikings game," she said.

Martin said her trip home with her daughter will take even longer. Delta booked them to Memphis, and her husband will drive the four hours from Birmingham to pick them up.

Shortly after the groundstop, the longest since 9/11, Duffy pushed Congress for as much as $50 billion for upgrades to the aging U.S. air traffic control system. A day later, Democratic lawmakers pushed back, citing unspent money from existing funding, which includes Congress's approval last year of $12.5 billion for airport and air traffic control improvements over five years.

"Our committee, on behalf of the flying public, will hold the administration accountable for what happened to make sure this chaos does not happen again," said Reps. Rick Larsen (D.-Wash.), ranking member of the House Committee on Transportation and Infrastructure, and Rep. Andre Carson (D.-Ind.), ranking member of the aviation subcommittee, said in a joint statement.

#2760. Gilgeous-Alexander scores 37 as Thunder beat Kings 125-103 to snap 3-game losing streak

SACRAMENTO, Calif. (AP) — Shai Gilgeous-Alexander scored 37 points in three quarters to help the Oklahoma City Thunder beat the Sacramento Kings 125-103 on Sunday night and end a three-game losing streak.

Gilgeous-Alexander shot 13 for 20 from the field, 4 for 5 from 3-point range and made all seven free throws. He played only 29 minutes, perhaps the only thing keeping him from a second consecutive game with 40 or more points.

He has scored 31 or more in his last three games.

Rookie Aday Mara had 16 points and 12 rebounds off the bench for the Thunder, who led by 27 points. Ajay Mitchell had 14 points, and Isaiah Hartenstein pulled down 11 rebounds.

Rookie Darius Acuff Jr. had 26 points and six rebounds for the Kings, who fell to 1-12 and are 0-8 at home. They fell to 0-6 on a seven-game homestand. Zach LaVine did not suit up for the Kings due to an illness.

After trailing 25-11, the Kings managed to charge back to tie the game at 47 on a free throw by Acuff with 3:42 left in the second quarter. The Thunder ended the period on a 15-3 run to lead 62-50 at the half after eight lead changes.

Gilgeous-Alexander had 21 points in 18 minutes at the break.

Gilgeous-Alexander's 3-pointer gave the Thunder a 91-76 advantage with 1:44 remaining in the third quarter. The Thunder opened the fourth on a 19-4 run while Gilgeous-Alexander was watching from the bench and he did not return to the game.

Nique Clifford scored nine points in his return to Sacramento after the Kings traded him to the Thunder on Nov. 8.

#2759. Japan's economy unexpectedly contracts in third-quarter as elevated energy costs bite

Japan's economy unexpectedly contracted in the third quarter, shrinking at an annualized 0.1% compared to the previous quarter, as elevated energy prices weighed on consumption and net exports.

A Reuters poll of economists had estimated the economy to grow at 0.3% in the three months to September, while GDP had expanded at 1.4% in the previous quarter.

Japan's exports beat expectations for two of the three months in the quarter, but net exports weighed on the economy due to higher costs of energy imports, cutting 0.6 percentage point from the country's growth.

Energy subsidies for households had reportedly expired in September, exposing consumers to higher energy prices brought about by the Iran war.

The GDP data could complicate Japan's policy normalization path, though there's still a 75.6% chance of the Bank of Japan raising rates by 25 basis points in December, according to LSEG.

"Today's GDP data is hardly a green light for the BOJ to hike in December," according to Krishna Bhimavarapu, APAC economist at State Street Investment Management.

He pointed out that real final sales of domestic product fell 2.1% annualized, which is the largest decline outside the Covid-19 pandemic and the first quarter of 2022. "[This] looks to be a loss of momentum," he added. That metric is a measure of

Real final sales of domestic product refer to GDP growth, net of private inventories, and indicate underlying demand.

Marcel Thieliant, head of Asia Pacific at Capital Economics, still expects the BOJ to go ahead with the rate hike. "It looks increasingly likely that the Bank will have to tighten policy more aggressively than we've been anticipating as inflation is about to surge." The BOJ had declared that its 2% underlying inflation target had been met for the first time in 13 years at its meeting last month. Core inflation in September also hit its highest level this year at 2.1%, overshooting analysts' expectations.

On an year-on-year basis Japan's economy grew 1.1%, its strongest growth since the second quarter of 2025. Theliant noted that this was owed to a lower base in the third quarter of 2025.

Following the data release, the Nikkei 225 fell 0.62%, while the yield on the benchmark 10-year Japanese Government Bonds was at 2.885%. The yen weakened marginally against the dollar, trading at 159.76.

#2758. Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return

Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

PG-13 was rated R in his return to Philadelphia.

The middle finger Paul George directed toward one baseline fan only reinforced that rating.

Tuesday night was expected to revolve around Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum sharing an NBA court as opponents for the first time. Instead, the other headliner in the summer's blockbuster trade stole the show.

George scored a game-high 36 points in Boston's 123-101 win over the Sixers. He shot 14 for 22 from the floor, made six of his 11 three-point attempts and scored 14 consecutive Boston points in the fourth quarter.

The game was tied at 79 with 3:19 remaining in the third quarter. Boston outscored Philadelphia 44-22 from that point forward.

The Celtics went nine minutes and 20 seconds without making a two-point field goal during the second half. Instead, they made nine consecutive field goals from three, the second-longest such streak by a Sixers opponent over the last 30 seasons.

Payton Pritchard opened the fourth with consecutive threes. George followed with eight straight points, helping the Celtics begin the quarter on a 14-0 run that turned a competitive game into a rout.

George's return carried a complicated history. His two seasons with the Sixers were repeatedly interrupted by injuries, but he also played a significant role in their most memorable playoff run in years.

Last spring, George helped the Sixers overcome a 3-1 deficit to eliminate Boston in seven games. He averaged 17.4 points and shot 55 percent from three during the series.

Tyrese Maxey was happy to see his former teammate Tuesday, even if he disliked what followed.

"It was good to see Paul," Maxey said. "I love him, love his family. … He gave us everything he had while he was here. We know what he did in the playoffs last year. Great person, great family, and we'll see him again."

Maxey then added one important qualifier.

George instead scored from nearly everywhere.

"He had some open threes. He had some pull-up threes. He had some middies," Maxey said. "He played within the game, too. That's what Paul does. He plays within the game. He takes what the defense gives him, and we gave him too many open looks tonight.

"PG, we know what PG does. We shouldn't even be allowing him to get up 11 threes, and that's on us."

Nick Nurse watched George attack the Sixers in several ways.

"He really had it going," Nurse said. "He'd post up, and-one, fallaways. He went down the lane, high archer off the glass, and-one. He scored in about every way he could tonight."

George heard boos during his return and said the arena carried the atmosphere, though not quite the intensity, of a playoff game.

"This building can be very electric as well," George said. "It was fun. It was fun to play in this environment."

One interaction extended beyond the usual reception for a returning player.

During a stoppage, the ball rolled toward a fan seated along the baseline. George said the fan looked directly at him before dropping the ball instead of returning it.

"There was a fan being an a–," George said. "The ball rolled to him. He looked me dead in my eyes, dropped the ball on the ground as if I'm a dog to go fetch or go get it, instead of passing the ball to me like a normal fan would do.

"And so, yeah, then I rolled him a middle finger in return."

#2756. Trump breaks silence about FAA nationwide ground stop, blames Biden administration

President Donald Trump on Sunday made his first public comments about Friday's nationwide ground stop by the Federal Aviation Administration, blaming the Biden administration for making an "old and broken" air traffic control system worse. Trump also urged Congress to approve more funding to modernize the system.

The ground stop lasted more than four hours Friday, disrupted flights nationwide, contributed to thousands of cancellations and delays, and affected hundreds of thousands of passengers.

"The Biden Administration was an FAA disaster," Trump wrote on his social media platform. "Big money spent unwisely, making an old and broken system worse than when they began."

The president added that his administration has "the knowledge and plan for the Flight Control Systems," but said that "we need Congress to give the money, FAST!"

Congress approved $12.5 billion for the FAA last year as part of a major tax and spending package. Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy has said that the aging air traffic control system will need more funding for a broader overhaul.

The FAA said its equipment became unstable after a high volume of data flooded its network. The agency said contractor work at one site may be related and that the underlying cause remains under investigation.

Nationwide FAA ground stops are rare. Before the Nov. 2026 shutdown, the last two were after the Sept. 11 attacks and a brief FAA computer outage in January 2023.

#2755. Spurs streak to 12th win, Lakers roll on

The San Antonio Spurs were forced to dig deep before defeating the Utah Jazz 110-102 to clinch their 12th straight victory on Sunday as the Los Angeles Lakers extended their winning streak to eight games.

Utah, languishing at the bottom of the Western Conference with only one win, had threatened to pull off a huge upset after leading by five points midway through the fourth quarter.

But San Antonio closed with a 16-4 run in the final three minutes to improve to 12-2 at the top of the Western Conference standings, neck-and-neck with the Lakers.

Dylan Harper led San Antonio's scorers with 31 points, while French star Victor Wembanyama had another big game at both ends with 26 points, 10 rebounds, six assists and five blocks.

Los Angeles kept pace with their rivals from Texas on Sunday after Luka Doncic delivered his seventh 40-point performance of the season in a 131-123 road win over the Phoenix Suns.

Doncic, who is averaging 39 points per game, drained five three-pointers en route to 43 points with nine assists in a wire-to-wire victory.

The Lakers had appeared to be strolling towards a rout after surging into a 31-point lead late in the third quarter.

But they were given a huge scare in the fourth quarter as Phoenix threatened a sensational comeback after closing the gap to just five points.

Despite being outscored 43-27 in the final frame, the Lakers held off the rally to move to 12-2 in the Western Conference.

"We got a little bit sloppy in that fourth quarter so we've really got to work on that," Doncic said.

"I know we've got eight in a row, but we've got to do better at that."

With the Lakers still missing the injured Austin Reaves, Doncic was given scoring support from Quentin Grimes, who rattled in five three-pointers in a 22-point haul.

Collin Sexton added 16 from the bench, and Walker Kessler finished with a double-double of 14 points and 12 rebounds.

The Oklahoma City Thunder snapped their three-game losing streak. Shai Gilgeous-Alexander scored 37 in a 125-103 win at Sacramento.

In the Eastern Conference, LeBron James moved into third place on the NBA's all-time assists list as the Philadelphia 76ers overpowered the Indiana Pacers 131-124.

James finished with 15 points and 11 assists as the Sixers improved to 11-3.

James is now one ahead of Jason Kidd's 12,091 career assists. Chris Paul is second on the list with 12,552, while John Stockton leads with 15,806.

"It's something that I didn't obviously foresee in my career, but assists mean a lot to me -- moving the ball, being able to find my team-mates.

"And just being linked to some of the greatest to ever play this game, and also a dear friend of mine in JK (Kidd). It's very special," James added.

The Sixers remain half a game behind the Boston Celtics at the top of the Eastern Conference. The Celtics cruised to a sixth straight win on Sunday with a 131-94 rout of the Dallas Mavericks at the TD Garden.

Jayson Tatum led the way with 32 points, six rebounds and six assists as the Celtics improved to 11-2.

The reigning NBA champion New York Knicks improved to 7-6 after a 112-108 overtime win against the Toronto Raptors.

Karl-Anthony Towns and Jalen Brunson each scored 28 points for New York.

#2754. China’s slowdown deepens in October as industrial output, retail sales, investment miss estimates

China's economic slowdown worsened in October with retail sales, investment and industrial output all missing economists' estimates, as analysts warn a growing reliance on external demand leaves growth more exposed to shocks abroad.

Monday's data showed the investment slump deepened and retail sales again lagged industrial output, as consumer spending languished with the property market downturn eroding household wealth for a fifth year.

Retail sales, a gauge of consumption, rose 0.4% from a year ago in October, disappointing analysts' forecast for a 0.8% rise, slowing from a six-month high of 1.1% in September.

Industrial output expanded 4.8% from a year earlier last month, missing analysts' estimates for 5.3% growth and sharply slowing from 5.5% in September, the highest level in five months.

For the first ten months of this year, fixed-asset investments, including property and infrastructure, shrank 8.1%, deepening a collapse plaguing the economy this year that's unprecedented outside the Covid-19 period. That figure marked a significant deterioration compared to analysts' expectations for a 7% decline.

The economic malaise also weighed on the labor market. Unemployment inched lower to 5.1% in October, while the jobless rate for those aged between 16 and 24, excluding students, hovered near the highest level in a year, standing at 18% in September.

Growth in the world's second-largest economy bounced off three-year lows in the third quarter, expanding 4.5% from a year earlier. Goldman Sachs expects China's economy to slow to 4.6% this year, from 5% expansion in 2025, before easing further to 4.5% in 2027.

The Wall Street bank expects the low inflation to extend into next year, with consumer inflation ticking lower to 0.8% this year, 0.5 in 2027. It cited soft domestic demand, abundant aggregate supply, easing commodity-price momentum and potential artificial intelligence disruption risks.

Despite the moderation, the projected growth rate still puts the economy on track to achieve Beijing's ambition of doubling 2020 per capita income by 2035, Goldman Sachs said in a note Monday.

Subdued consumer inflation and roaring exports paint a picture of an economy increasingly dominant in global markets but struggling to generate prosperity at home. Consumer inflation ticked down to 0.7% in October, its first deceleration after two straight months of increases, while exports surged by a more-than-expected 35.2%.

China is on track to surpass last year's record $1.2 trillion trade surplus despite growing trade tensions, with the gap reaching the $1 trillion threshold by October — the fastest it has ever reached that mark.

The strong export momentum has largely been driven by global demand for AI-related technology and equipment. Any signs of pullback in the industry could weigh on China's exports, analysts warn.

"The market was already worried" about a slowdown in the AI capital-spending cycle even before the Nvidia scandal that put flows of restricted chips to China under renewed scrutiny, said Zhiwei Zhang, president and chief economist at Pinpoint Asset Management.

"Now the risk has become even more serious," Zhang said, forecasting further downside risk for global economic growth next year.

The increasingly lopsided growth came as major trading partners have ramped up calls for the economy to rebalance. Headwinds on China's export engine could intensify next year, with further trade restrictions from the European Union posing a critical downside risk, Citi Research analysts said.

"More EU trade measures targeting China look plausible to us, potentially adding to tensions ahead," the bank said, though Beijing is likely to be open to negotiating "reasonable, practical concessions," such as import pledges, sector-specific deals and value-added tax rebate cuts.

— CNBC's Evelyn Cheng contributed to this report.

#2753. Winners and Losers from Seahawks vs. Raiders: It’s JSN. It’s always JSN.

The Seattle Seahawks had 30 minutes to arguably save their season after falling behind 10-7 to the Las Vegas Raiders at halftime. Given the deep nature of the NFC playoff picture, Seattle had little margin for error to lose too many more games, and especially not versus inferior (on paper) opposition. Mike Macdonald's team locked in after halftime and got the win they needed to stay afloat in the playoff race heading into their bye week.

Let's get to Winners and Losers from Seahawks 21, Raiders 13!

You can take a TD away from JSN through a Super Bowl XL-esque "pushoff" penalty, but you can't hold him down forever. JSN caught 8 passes for 171 yards and a 71-yard TD, and that's with two explosive plays wiped out due to penalty and a drop. He could've easily broken 200 without much effort, which is ridiculous regardless of opposition.

JSN is on pace for a 2,000-yard season and he has the Triple Crown lead over the rest of the NFL in yards, receptions, and touchdowns. When you're the best in the business in a deep field of elite receivers, you're special. JSN is one of a kind.

Cooper Kupp does something he's never done before

It's fitting that Seattle's two slot machines scored touchdowns in Las Vegas. Kupp's lone catch was a sliding grab for 34 yards on a scramble drill, marking the first time he's ever caught a touchdown from a backup quarterback in his NFL career. That sounds nerdy and is partially down to the durability of Matthew Stafford, Jared Goff, and (well, at least until this season) Sam Darnold, but even great WRs occasionally get a garbage time TD from the backup.

For the first time in his career, Jadarian Price is an NFL touchdown scorer. He had nine carries for 36 yards, showcasing his speed on his 12-yard touchdown run. Price also had a big kick return to start the game, although it was wasted on an opening drive fumble. There are ways for Price to get his touches without being the lead back, and he adds another splash play to his reel in the form of a TD.

Zach Charbonnet gets some hard-earned yards

The blocking wasn't always there, but Charbonnet was on the cusp of his first 100-yard game since returning from injury. He had 94 yards on 20 carries, with several Raiders defenders left in the dust after he chucked them off of him. What sets him apart from Jadarian Price is his ability to drive forward after first contact more effectively than the rookie, and that's one of the reason he'll be RB1 moving forward.

Four games played, four wins for Drew Lock. In fact, when you add in his two starts in 2023 he has a 4-1 record as a starter and an unofficial 5-1 record as Seahawks QB. Lock was 14/17 for 240 yards and 2 TDs, and his numbers would've been better if not for a couple of negated plays via penalty. The first fumble was sloppy and not a smart play when a sack is totally fine, but the second fumble was unlucky. His biggest moment was lowering the shoulder and calling game on a bootleg run in the final minutes.

Whether Lock starts after the bye or not, the important thing is that he's comfortable in the offense and taking advantage of defenses he should be taking advantage of. He's going to get significant money this offseason, whether it's Seattle believing he might be the new long-term answer or some other team that sees Drew as a high-end bridge or "the next Sam Darnold" of sorts. This is the best Lock has ever been arguably in his entire career, irrespective of opponent.

So what if Spoon doesn't get interceptions? When you have a cornerback who can play slot or outside, defend the run as well as defend the pass, and blitz quarterbacks better than most linebackers, you've got yourself one hell of a chess piece. Witherspoon sacked Fernando Mendoza twice and is a total menace when anything is near the line of scrimmage.

Big Murph has five sacks on the season, which leads the Seahawks. He wanted to reach 10 in his third year and he's halfway there. Murphy's run defense hasn't always been strong this season, but he continues leveling up as a pass rusher.

Dante Fowler picked up his second sack in as many weeks, coming in unblocked on a blitz when Nick Emmanwori attacked the inside. DeMarcus Lawrence and Derick Hall both had sacks and several pressures of Fernando Mendoza. Fowler having any sort of positive contribution is welcome given his underwhelming start to the season, and if he's going to keep having a low snap count then he might as well maximize his opportunities.

Seattle's secondary blankets Raiders' outmatched receivers

The Raiders don't have a quality wide receiver group, with all due respect to Cody White and his one catch for 22 yards. When targeting wide receivers, Mendoza was 5/14 for 40 yards. Josh Jobe in particular had a fantastic performance and chipped in with two TFLs.

Tory Horton's 5D chess is part genius, part athletic brilliance

For reasons already stated in this article, Horton basically cost the Raiders their two-minute warning timeout by saving a touchback. He channeled his inner Richard Sherman with "The Tip" to Rashid Shaheed, albeit one that's an illegal bat by rule, and there went the Raiders' ideal outcome. That was impressive.

Bobby Hart gets the job done with Abe Lucas out

I was a little worried when Bobby Hart had to sub in for Abe Lucas after his ankle injury. Hart's last season starting with the Los Angeles Chargers was… brutal. Hart didn't give up any pressures that I saw. If you can sub in and have to deal with Maxx Crosby and not be a disaster, that's a win.

Myers has missed at least a field goal or an extra point in four of his last seven games, and it's not like 44 yards in an indoor facility is a difficult kick. I'm a little bit antsy with Myers and how he's performed recently and particularly a contract year.

The Seahawks were so injured that even the team doctor got hurt

Abe Lucas, George Holani, and Nick Kallerup all left and didn't return due to their respective injuries. Nick Emmanwori re-aggravated a shoulder injury, although he says he's all good. Jarran Reed left with a toe injury and later returned despite an initial doubtful designation. Even Seattle's team doctor then got whacked on the head by a wayward Fernando Mendoza throw, although thankfully he's alright.

Gaining Quenton Nelson, Charles Cross, Sam Darnold, and (hopefully) Leonard Williams after the bye may be offset by what happened on Sunday. Hopefully the bye week is an ideal one-week buffer for any players who only have short-term injuries.

Mike Macdonald ratcheted up the pressure once the first couple of Raiders drives finished. Nothing can get a rookie's clock sped up faster than dealing with all sorts of rushers coming right at him.

The playing surface was unacceptable. Rolling in a natural grass surface for an indoor environment (or quasi-indoor like the Arizona Cardinals stadium) is not new but that was no different than playing at a random city park. So much slipping and sliding and uneven footing made it such a nervy game to watch.

Don't overlook Rashid Shaheed's 3rd down conversion. His only catch kept Seattle's opening second half drive alive and resulted in an eventual touchdown. Shaheed was also wide open on JSN's TD but Lock turned him down for a bigger play.

Brian Fleury called a much better 2nd half than 1st half. And what I mean is he didn't make literally all but one first down playcall a pass like he did in the first 30 minutes. That was wild to see Seattle get overly obsessed with establishing the run to the point where Drew Lock had five pass attempts by halftime. That naked bootleg run by Lock was arguably the best Fleury call to date, and the offense lit up the Raiders in the third quarter. No, he's not getting fired at the bye week.

Seattle didn't get a ton of help in the playoff chase, but Week 12 just became as high leverage as can be. The Seahawks have a must-win against the San Francisco 49ers, which could potentially put them back within a game of the division lead if the 49ers lose to the Minnesota Vikings next Sunday. But the other aspect of Week 12 is the Green Bay Packers (6-4) playing the Los Angeles Rams (6-4) on Thanksgiving Eve, and the outcome of that game essentially determines Seattle's pecking order in the playoff hunt entering December. Seattle still hasn't played its best football of the season (at least I don't think), so get healthy during the bye and figure out how to attack this stretch run. At worst, I think Seattle needs to be 11-6 to reach the postseason. It's doable, but the Super Bowl champs have to find a way to get back to playing complementary football again regardless of who the starting QB is.
